=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array nums of n integers, and a 2D integer array queries of size q, where queries[i] = [l_i, r_i].
For each query, you must find the maximum XOR score of any subarray of nums[l_i..r_i].
The XOR score of an array a is found by repeatedly applying the following operations on a so that only one element remains, that is the score:

Simultaneously replace a[i] with a[i] XOR a[i + 1] for all indices i except the last one.
Remove the last element of a.

Return an array answer of size q where answer[i] is the answer to query i.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,8,4,32,16,1], queries = [[0,2],[1,4],[0,5]]
Output: [12,60,60]
Explanation:
In the first query, nums[0..2] has 6 subarrays [2], [8], [4], [2, 8], [8, 4], and [2, 8, 4] each with a respective XOR score of 2, 8, 4, 10, 12, and 6. The answer for the query is 12, the largest of all XOR scores.
In the second query, the subarray of nums[1..4] with the largest XOR score is nums[1..4] with a score of 60.
In the third query, the subarray of nums[0..5] with the largest XOR score is nums[1..4] with a score of 60.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [0,7,3,2,8,5,1], queries = [[0,3],[1,5],[2,4],[2,6],[5,6]]
Output: [7,14,11,14,5]
Explanation:



Index
nums[l_i..r_i]
Maximum XOR Score Subarray
Maximum Subarray XOR Score




0
[0, 7, 3, 2]
[7]
7


1
[7, 3, 2, 8, 5]
[7, 3, 2, 8]
14


2
[3, 2, 8]
[3, 2, 8]
11


3
[3, 2, 8, 5, 1]
[2, 8, 5, 1]
14


4
[5, 1]
[5]
5




 
Constraints:

1 <= n == nums.length <= 2000
0 <= nums[i] <= 2^31 - 1
1 <= q == queries.length <= 10^5
queries[i].length == 2 
queries[i] = [l_i, r_i]
0 <= l_i <= r_i <= n - 1

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We have array nums length n <= 2000. q up to 1e5. Need answer range maximum "XOR score" of any subarray within nums[l..r]. Need understand operation.

Given array a length m. Repeatedly simultaneously replace a[i] with a[i] XOR a[i+1] for i=0..m-2, remove last. After one step length m-1, new b_i = a_i xor a_{i+1}. After m-1 steps one element. What is final value in terms of original? This is like Pascal triangle mod 2 XOR. For array a length m, final = XOR over i of a_i if binomial coefficient C(m-1, i) odd? Let's verify.

Operation is adjacent XOR, linear over GF(2). After k steps, element at position i (0-based) equals XOR of original a_j for j in [i, i+k] with coefficient C(k, j-i) mod 2? Let's derive: applying difference operator (1 + shift) k times: coefficient C(k, t) mod2. Final after m-1 steps at position 0: XOR_{j=0}^{m-1} (C(m-1, j) mod2) * a_j. Yes.

By Lucas theorem, C(m-1, j) odd iff j bitwise subset of m-1 (i.e., (j & ~(m-1)) == 0). Thus XOR score of subarray nums[l..r] length len = r-l+1, k=len-1: score = XOR of nums[l+j] for all j subset of k (0<=j<=k). Equivalently XOR over positions p = l + j where (p-l) & ~(r-l) == 0? Since k = r-l. Wait m-1 = r-l = len-1. Let d = r-l. Score = XOR_{j subset d} nums[l+j]. So for any subarray [x..y] within query [l..r], score = XOR_{j subset (y-x)} nums[x+j]. Need maximum over all x,y with l<=x<=y<=r.

Implementation plan:
from typing import List
class Solution:
    def maximumSubarrayXor(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        q = len(queries)
        ans = [0]*q
        # group queries by length (r-l+1)
        by_len = [[] for _ in range(n+1)]
        for idx, (l,r) in enumerate(queries):
            length = r-l+1
            by_len[length].append((l, idx))
        # length 1
        prevF = nums[:]  # F for current? Actually for len=1
        prevAns = nums[:]  # ans for len=1
        for l, idx in by_len[1]:
            ans[idx] = prevAns[l]  # nums[l]
        # For len from 2 to n:
        for length in range(2, n+1):
            m = n - length + 1
            curF = [0]*m
            curAns = [0]*m
            # F[l][l+length-1] = prevF[l] ^ prevF[l+1] where prevF length n-(length-1)+1 = n-length+2? For length=2, prevF length n, m=n-1, need prevF[l+1] up to n-1, okay.
            # But prevF length = n - (length-1) + 1 = n - length + 2. curF length m = n - length +1. Need loop l in 0..m-1, prevF[l+1] exists max index m = n-length+1, prevF length n-length+2, yes.
            for l in range(m):
                val = prevF[l] ^ prevF[l+1]
                curF[l] = val
                # prevAns length same as prevF length? For length-1, ans for intervals length-1: indices 0..n-(length-1) = n-length+1? Wait prevAns length = n - (length-1) + 1 = n - length + 2. curAns length m = n - length +1. prevAns[l] and prevAns[l+1] exist (l+1 <= n-length+1). Good.
                a = prevAns[l]
                b = prevAns[l+1]
                best = val if val >= a else a
                if b > best: best = b
                curAns[l] = best
            # answer queries of this length
            if by_len[length]:
                for l, idx in by_len[length]:
                    ans[idx] = curAns[l]
            prevF = curF
            prevAns = curAns
        return ans

Let's derive F recurrence in another way to ensure no off-by-one:
For array a length m, score S_m(a) = XOR_{j=0}^{m-1} C(m-1,j) a_j mod2.
Then S_m(a) = XOR_{j=0}^{m-2} C(m-2,j)(a_j xor a_{j+1})? Wait after one operation, b length m-1, S_m(a)=S_{m-1}(b) = XOR_{i=0}^{m-2} C(m-2,i) b_i = XOR_i C(m-2,i)(a_i xor a_{i+1}) = XOR_j [C(m-2,j)+C(m-2,j-1)] a_j = XOR_j C(m-1,j) a_j. This is true. But recurrence F(l,r) = F(l,r-1) XOR F(l+1,r):
F(l,r-1) = XOR_{j=0}^{m-2} C(m-2,j) a_{l+j}
F(l+1,r) = XOR_{j=0}^{m-2} C(m-2,j) a_{l+1+j}
XOR = XOR_{j=0}^{m-2} C(m-2,j)(a_{l+j} xor a_{l+1+j}) = S_{m-1}(b) = S_m(a). So yes.

Wait, the recurrence F(l,r) = F(l,r-1) XOR F(l+1,r) is exactly S_m(a) = S_{m-1}(prefix) XOR S_{m-1}(suffix). But S_{m-1}(prefix) = F(l,r-1), S_{m-1}(suffix)=F(l+1,r). Good.

Now, let's think about possible improvements to code style:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumSubarrayXor(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        res = [0] * len(queries)
        by_len = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
        for qi, (l, r) in enumerate(queries):
            by_len[r - l + 1].append((l, qi))

        prev_f = nums[:]      # score of intervals of current length - 1
        prev_best = nums[:]   # best answer inside intervals of current length - 1

        for l, qi in by_len[1]:
            res[qi] = prev_best[l]

        for length in range(2, n + 1):
            m = n - length + 1
            cur_f = [0] * m
            cur_best = [0] * m
            for l in range(m):
                score = prev_f[l] ^ prev_f[l + 1]
                best = score
                if prev_best[l] > best:
                    best = prev_best[l]
                if prev_best[l + 1] > best:
                    best = prev_best[l + 1]
                cur_f[l] = score
                cur_best[l] = best

            for l, qi in by_len[length]:
                res[qi] = cur_best[l]

            prev_f = cur_f
            prev_best = cur_best

        return res
```

Let's maybe prove ans recurrence:
For interval I=[l,r], max over subarrays J⊆I. If J=I, score=F[l][r]. Otherwise J has left >l or right <r (or both). If left>l, J⊆[l+1,r], so score≤ans[l+1][r]. If right<r, J⊆[l,r-1], score≤ans[l][r-1]. Conversely ans[l+1][r] and ans[l][r-1] are scores of subarrays within I, so max of these and F is correct. Good.

Let's think about if there is any issue with "XOR score" of an array a is found by repeatedly applying operations on a so that only one element remains, that is the score" If array length m, after m
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
>
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def maximumSubarrayXor(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        ans = [0] * len(queries)

        # Group queries by interval length.
        by_len = [[] for _ in range(n + 1)]
        for qi, (l, r) in enumerate(queries):
            by_len[r - l + 1].append((l, qi))

        # Length 1: score and best are just the single element.
        prev_score = nums[:]
        prev_best = nums[:]

        for l, qi in by_len[1]:
            ans[qi] = prev_best[l]

        # Compute increasing interval lengths.
        for length in range(2, n + 1):
            m = n - length + 1
            cur_score = [0] * m
            cur_best = [0] * m

            for l in range(m):
                # score[l][r] = score[l][r-1] ^ score[l+1][r]
                score = prev_score[l] ^ prev_score[l + 1]

                # best[l][r] = max(score[l][r], best[l][r-1], best[l+1][r])
                best = score
                if prev_best[l] > best:
                    best = prev_best[l]
                if prev_best[l + 1] > best:
                    best = prev_best[l + 1]

                cur_score[l] = score
                cur_best[l] = best

            for l, qi in by_len[length]:
                ans[qi] = cur_best[l]

            prev_score = cur_score
            prev_best = cur_best

        return ans
```
